\displaystyle\oint d^{3}x\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Big[bW(10\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\kappa^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa^{rB}+\kappa^{AB}\kappa_{AB})

\displaystyle+4b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}-4b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\kappa_{AB}\kappa^{rB}-\kappa_{B}^{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\kappa^{rB}+\kappa^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa_{A}^{r}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{A}^{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa^{rB}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\Big[\frac{1}{4}bW\big(2\theta^{2}-7\theta_{AB}\theta^{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta^{BC}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta-2\theta^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\big)-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\theta_{BC}\theta^{BC}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W\big(\theta^{2}-3\theta_{BC}\theta^{BC}\big)+\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}W\big(\theta_{A}^{C}\theta_{CB}-\theta_{AB}\theta\big)\Big]\,.

(86)

These non-linear terms are quadratic functions of the charges
U
and
V
of the subleading supertranslations. Our task now is to translate the above formulas in terms of the brackets of the generators.

Brackets involving the homogeneous Lorentz generators

We then find from the linear terms in Subsection
4.2.1
and from (
83
) that the brackets of the homogeneous Lorentz generators read

\displaystyle\{B^{i},B^{j}\}

\displaystyle=2M^{ij}+\Lambda^{ij}\,,

(87)

\displaystyle\{B^{i},M^{jk}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\delta^{ik}B^{j}-\delta^{ij}B^{k}\Big)\,,

(88)

\displaystyle\{M^{ij},M^{kl}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\delta^{ik}M^{jl}-\delta^{il}M^{jk}-\delta^{jk}M^{il}+\delta^{jl}M^{ik}\Big)\,.

(89)

where the non-linear term
\Lambda^{ij}
is cubic in the supertranslation generators and given by

\Lambda^{ij}=2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,x^{[i}e^{j]A}\Big[8K_{A}K_{B}+2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)+\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)^{2}\Big]K^{B}\,.

(90)

There is no non-linear contribution in the brackets involving the angular momentum
M^{ij}
.

The brackets of the subleading supertranslation generators
(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3)U
and
K_{A}
with the homogeneous Lorentz generators are linear. They read

\displaystyle\{B^{i},(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3)U\}

\displaystyle=-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(n^{i}K_{A})\,,

(91)

\displaystyle\{M^{ij},(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3)U\}

\displaystyle=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\big[x^{[i}e^{j]A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3)U\big]\,,

(92)

\displaystyle\{B^{i},K_{A}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3)U\big]+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)\,,

(93)

\displaystyle\{M^{ij},K_{A}\}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(x^{[i}e^{j]B}K_{A})-x^{[i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}e^{j]B}K_{B}\,.

(94)

They are equivalent, up to relevant projections and unphysical ambiguities, to the following Lorentz brackets of
U
and
V
,

\displaystyle\{B^{i},U\}

\displaystyle=-2n^{i}V\,,

(95)

\displaystyle\{M^{ij},U\}

\displaystyle=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(x^{[i}e^{j]B}U)\,,

(96)

\displaystyle\{B^{i},V\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big[4n^{i}U+\partial_{A}n^{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}U+n^{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U\Big]\,,

(97)

\displaystyle\{M^{ij},V\}

\displaystyle=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(x^{[i}e^{j]B}V)\,.

(98)

The brackets of the leading supertranslations with the homogeneous Lorentz generators read

\displaystyle\{B^{i},\mathcal{T}\}

\displaystyle=-n^{i}\mathcal{W}+\Lambda^{i}_{\mathcal{T}}\,,

(99)

\displaystyle\{M^{ij},\mathcal{T}\}

\displaystyle=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(x^{[i}e^{j]B}\mathcal{T})\,,

(100)

\displaystyle\{B^{i},\mathcal{W}\}

\displaystyle=-4n^{i}\mathcal{T}-\partial_{A}n^{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\mathcal{T}-n^{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\mathcal{T}+\Lambda^{i}_{\mathcal{W}}\,,

(101)

\displaystyle\{M^{ij},\mathcal{W}\}

\displaystyle=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(x^{[i}e^{j]B}\mathcal{W})\,.

(102)

The non-linear contributions
\Lambda^{i}_{\mathcal{T}}
and
\Lambda^{i}_{\mathcal{W}}
are given by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}}^{i}

\displaystyle=K^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)+K^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)]

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}[K_{B}n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}U)]+3\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}[K^{A}n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)]\,,

(103)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}^{i}

\displaystyle=10n^{i}K^{A}K_{A}-n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}K^{C})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+n^{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}K^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}K_{B}+4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}K^{C}K^{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}K^{B}K_{B}-\frac{1}{2}K_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}K^{B})]

\displaystyle\quad+4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}(n^{i}K^{A}K_{A})+\frac{1}{4}n^{i}\Big[2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)]^{2}-7(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}U)

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}U)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)\Big]+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\Big[\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}U+\delta_{A}^{C}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)\Big]\,.

(104)

Brackets of the supertranslation generators

The supertranslation generators form a subalgebra, which we can read off by collecting the information from the above formulas. We find that the non-vanishing brackets are

\displaystyle\{Q_{T_{1}},Q_{T_{2}}\}

\displaystyle=Q_{{\hat{I}^{(1)}}_{\{T_{1},T_{2}\}}}=-2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(T_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}T_{2}-T_{2}D_{A}T_{1})V\,,

(105)

\displaystyle\{Q_{W},Q_{T^{(1)}}\}

\displaystyle=2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,W\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3\right)T^{(1)}=2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,W\,\tilde{T}^{(1)}\,,

(106)

\displaystyle\{Q_{T},Q_{I^{(1)}}\}

\displaystyle=-2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,I^{(1)}T\,.

(107)

with

\hat{I}^{(1)}_{\{T_{1},T_{2}\}}=T_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}T_{2}-T_{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}T_{1}

(108)

The algebra of the leading and subleading supertranslations is thus non abelian and centrally extended. The non-abelian commutator is
\{Q_{T_{1}},Q_{T_{2}}\}\sim Q_{{\hat{I}^{(1)}}}
, showing that the Poisson bracket of two leading supertranslations in time yields a subleading supertranslation in space.

This is not too surprising, in view of the fact that the Lie bracket of two vector fields of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
will generically be a vector field of order
\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})
. We should stress, however, that the supertranslations are accompanied by field-dependent correcting terms. These terms play an important role in the specific form of the algebra encountered above. They are in particular key in the emergence of the central charges.

The presence of
Q_{{\hat{I}^{(1)}}_{\{T_{1},T_{2}\}}}
in
\{Q_{T_{1}},Q_{T_{2}}\}
was overlooked in
Fuentealba:2021yvo
, where the abelian relation
\{Q_{T_{1}},Q_{T_{2}}\}=0
was given instead. It turns out that one can add field-dependent diffeomorphisms to
T
such that the leading supertranslations commute. This is done in the next section. With the choice made above leading to the definite expression (
64
) for
Q_{T}
, however, one gets
\{Q_{T_{1}},Q_{T_{2}}\}\not=0
.

How to simplify the asymptotic symmetry algebra by non-linear redefinitions of the generators is a question to which we now turn.

5
Simplification of the presentation of the BMS
5
algebra

5.1
Non-linear algebras and Poisson manifolds

A striking feature of the above charge algebra is that it is non-linear. In particular, the bracket of two boost generators is cubic in the charges of the subleading supertranslations.

That symmetry charges might form a non-linear algebra is not a surprise. It is in fact more the rule than the exception, as clearly discussed in
deBoer:1995cqx
. In terms of the canonical transformations generated by the charges, non-linearities mean that the commutator of the corresponding transformations is a transformation that takes the same form, but with coefficients that are functions of the fields through the charges. Indeed, if
Q_{a}
is a complete set of charges with
\{Q_{a},Q_{b}\}=f_{ab}(Q)
and
X_{a}
the corresponding vector fields (
X_{a}F=\{Q_{a},F\}
), then one finds
[X_{a},X_{b}]=X_{\{Q_{b},Q_{a}\}}=X_{f_{ba}(Q)}=\frac{\partial f_{ba}}{\partial Q_{c}}X_{c}
. This is not a linearly independent vector field (over the functions), just as the corresponding charge-generator
f_{ab}
is not independent from the
Q_{a}
’s.

Thus, in our case, the non-linearities found in the BMS
5
brackets simply imply that the parameters
T^{(1)}
,
W^{(1)}
and
I^{(1)}_{A}
associated with the commutator of two asymptotic symmetries depend on the fields through the charges. As we just observed, such transformations do not define independent asymptotic symmetries. Examples of non-linear asymptotic symmetry algebras have been found earlier in
Henneaux:1999ib
;
Henneaux:2010xg
;
Campoleoni:2010zq
;
Afshar:2013vka
;
Gonzalez:2013oaa
;
Fuentealba:2015jma
;
Henneaux:2015ywa
;
Fuentealba:2015wza
;
Henneaux:2015tar
;
Fuentealba:2020zkf
;
Fuentealba:2021xhn
.

There is a great flexibility in the presentation of non-linear algebras since one can make non-linear redefinition of the charges, under which
f_{ab}(Q)
changes. That non-linear redefinitions may have a dramatic effect already in the Lie algebra case is illustrated in the recent paper
Rodriguez:2021tcz
.

The space generated by the symmetry charges is a Poisson manifold, about which much is known in the finite-dimensional case
Lichnerowicz
;
Weinstein
;
Arnold
. In particular, one can bring the Poisson bracket to a canonical form that generalizes the Darboux canonical form by (non-linear) redefinitions of the charges (“Weinstein splitting theorem” or “Darboux-Weinstein theorem”). Explicitly, one has

Theorem
Lichnerowicz
;
Weinstein
:
Let
P
be an arbitrary point of a Poisson manifold. One can find local coordinates (
q_{i},p_{i},y_{\alpha}
) in a neighborhood
U
of
P
with the properties:

\{q_{i},q_{j}\}=\{p_{i},p_{j}\}=\{q_{i},y_{\alpha}\}=\{p_{i},y_{\alpha}\}=0,\quad,\{q_{i},p_{j}\}=\delta_{ij}\quad\{y_{\alpha},y_{\beta}\}=F_{\alpha\beta}(y)

(109)

with

F_{\alpha\beta}(y)=0\qquad\textrm{at }\;P,

(110)

Here,
i,j=1,\cdots,s
,
\alpha,\beta=1,\cdots,r
,
2s+r=n
where
n
is the dimension of the Poisson manifold
M
.

We can assume
P
to have coordinates
q_{i}=p_{i}=y_{\alpha}=0
, so that the last condition is
F_{\alpha\beta}(y=0)=0
. If one expands
F_{\alpha\beta}
in Taylor series, one has

F_{\alpha\beta}={C^{\gamma}}_{\alpha\beta}y_{\gamma}+O(y^{2})

(111)

where the
{C^{\gamma}}_{\alpha\beta}
are easily verified to fulfill the Jacobi identity and are thus necessarily the structure constants of a Lie algebra. An interesting question is whether one can get rid of the non linear terms by redefinitions. This question is reviewed in
FernandesMonnier
and can be expressed as a problem of Lie algebra cohomology (at least for the formal linearization problem)
Weinstein
. In particular, if the algebra is semi-simple, one can absorb the non-linear terms through redefinitions.

In our case, however, the space of the symmetry charges is infinite-dimensional, and furthermore, it is natural to restrict the redefinitions so as to maintain the structure of the new generators to be

\int d^{4}x(\xi\mathcal{H}+\xi^{i}\mathcal{H}_{i})+Q_{\xi}[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}]

(112)

where
(\xi,\xi^{i})
has the asymptotic behaviour (
37
)-(
39
). Accordingly, the only redefinitions that we allow are field-dependent redefinitions of the coefficients
b
,
T
,
W
,
T^{(1)}
,
W^{(1)}
and
I_{A}^{(1)}
characterizing this asymptotic behaviour, in a way that keeps integrability of the charges. This makes the problem different from the finite-dimensional one.

It turns out to be possible, however, to make non-linear redefinitions of the above type such that (i) supertranslations commute up to central charges; and (ii) the Lorentz subalgebra is linearly realized.

5.2
Abelian description of the supertranslations (with central extension)

In order to make the supertranslations abelian, we redefine the leading supertranslations
T
by adding to them a subleading supertranslation with parameter

I_{(T)}^{(1)A}=\frac{T}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\kappa^{rA}=TK^{A}\,.

(113)

Note that this parameter maintains integrability. It modifies the leading time supertranslation
charge by a term proportional to the square
K^{A}K_{A}
of the subleading space supertranslation charges. Thus,
we now have

Q_{T}=\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,T\mathcal{T}\,,

(114)

where the new
\mathcal{T}
(still denoted in the same way) is given by

\mathcal{T}=2k-2e^{(2)}+\frac{1}{4}\theta^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\theta_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\,-1}\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}\,.

(115)

and one easily checks that
\{Q_{T_{1}},Q_{T_{2}}\}=0
.

The extra improper gauge transformation added to
T
does
not affect the remaining brackets between the other time and spatial supertranslation generators, which we take unchanged,

Q_{W}=\oint d^{3}x\,W\,\mathcal{W}\,,\qquad\mathcal{W}=2\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(\theta_{AB}\kappa^{rB})\Big)

(116)

Q_{T^{(1)}}=-\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,T^{(1)}\Big((\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3)U\Big)\,,\qquad Q_{I^{(1)}}=-2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}I^{(1)}V\,.

(117)

The final supertranslation algebra is thus abelian with non-trivial central terms given by

\displaystyle\{Q_{W},Q_{T^{(1)}}\}

\displaystyle=2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,W\,(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3)T^{(1)}\,,

(118)

\displaystyle\{Q_{T},Q_{I^{(1)}}\}

\displaystyle=-2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,I^{(1)}T\,,

(119)

while all the other Poisson brackets between supertranslation generators vanish.

On can view the elimination of the charge-dependent terms in the algebra of the supertranslations as an application of Darboux theorem since the central term defines a non-degenerate antisymmetric matrix.

5.3
Linear realization of the Lorentz subalgebra

In order to eliminate the cubic terms in the boost algebra, we add to the boosts a subleading supertranslation with parameters

\displaystyle T_{(b)}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=2b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\,-1}\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}-\frac{b}{8}\theta^{2}\,,

(120)

\displaystyle I_{(b)}^{(1)A}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\kappa^{rA}\theta\,.

(121)

Again, these parameters yield an integrable generator. The new boost charges receive the following cubic contribution in the
subleading supertranslation charges

\oint d^{3}xb\left(-\frac{2}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}\theta+\frac{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}{24}\theta^{3}\right)

and now read

\displaystyle B^{i}=\oint d^{3}x\,\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,n^{i}\Big[2k^{(2)}+\theta e^{(2)}-\frac{1}{24}\theta^{3}-\frac{1}{3}\theta^{A}_{C}\theta^{C}_{B}\theta^{B}_{A}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{2}\theta^{A}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}_{A}+\frac{4}{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big(\kappa^{r}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}+(\theta_{AB}-\frac{\theta}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB})\kappa^{rA}\kappa^{rB}\Big)\Big]

(122)

while the rotation generators remain unchanged,

M^{ij}=\oint d^{3}x\,x^{[i}e^{j]A}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{(2)r}_{A}\,.

(123)

The redefined boosts obey

\{Q_{b_{1}},Q_{b_{2}}\}=\delta_{b_{2}}Q_{b_{1}}=Q_{\hat{Y}}

(124)

where
Q_{\hat{Y}}=\oint d^{3}x\,2\hat{Y}^{A}\pi_{A}^{(2)r}
with

\hat{Y}^{A}=b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}b_{2}-b_{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}b_{1}
, or equivalently,

\{B^{i},B^{j}\}=2M^{ij}

(125)

with no non-linear contributions.
Therefore, the Lorentz algebra becomes linearly realized. That one can eliminate the non-linear terms in the Lorentz algebra is not too surprising either since it is a simple, finite-dimensional algebra.

One can summarize the redefinitions of the generators of the symmetry algebra by saying that the “more” terms in (
37
)-(
39
) should contain the improper gauge transformations (
113
),
(
120
) and (
121
) in addition to the already displayed correcting terms. The final expression for the supertranslation parameters corresponding to the new form of the algebra
is thus

\displaystyle\overline{I}^{A}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W+I_{(b)}^{A}\,,

(126)

T

\displaystyle=T+T_{(b)}\,,

(127)

W

\displaystyle=W\,,

(128)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$T$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=T^{(1)}+T_{(b,W)}^{(1)}\,,

(129)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$I$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{(1)A}

\displaystyle=I^{(1)A}+I_{(b,T,W)}^{(1)A}\,,

(130)

where

\displaystyle I_{(b)}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\kappa^{rA}\,,

(131)

\displaystyle T_{(b)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{b\theta}{2}\,,

(132)

\displaystyle T_{(b,W)}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\partial_{B}b+(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+3)^{-1}\left[\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\Big)\Big(\frac{b}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}+b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\,-1}\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}-\frac{b}{8}\theta^{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W\kappa^{rA}\,,

(133)

\displaystyle I_{(b,T,W)}^{(1)A}

\displaystyle=\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}+\theta_{B}^{A}\kappa^{rB}-\theta\kappa^{rA}\big)+\frac{T}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\kappa^{rA}-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}W\,.

(134)

The modification of the boost and leading supertranslation generators change the non-linear terms appearing in their Poisson bracket. One now finds

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\{b,T\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{\{T,b\}}=-\oint d^{3}xb\kappa^{rA}\left(\theta_{A}^{B}\partial_{B}T-\theta\partial_{A}T\right)\,,

(135)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{\{W,b\}}

(136)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\oint d^{3}x\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Big[bW(2\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\kappa^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa^{rB}+\kappa^{AB}\kappa_{AB})+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}

\displaystyle+4b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}-4b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\kappa_{AB}\kappa^{rB}-\kappa_{B}^{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\kappa^{rB}+\kappa^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa_{A}^{r}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{A}^{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa^{rB}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\Big[\frac{1}{4}bW\big(5\theta^{2}-7\theta_{AB}\theta^{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta^{BC}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta-2\theta^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\big)-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\theta_{BC}\theta^{BC}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W\big(2\theta^{2}-3\theta_{BC}\theta^{BC}\big)+\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}W\big(\theta_{A}^{C}\theta_{CB}-\theta_{AB}\theta\big)\Big]\,.

The Poisson brackets of of the redefined boost and supertranslation charges are thus

\displaystyle\{B^{i},\mathcal{T}\}

\displaystyle=-n^{i}\mathcal{W}+\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}}^{i}\,,

(137)

\displaystyle\{B^{i},\mathcal{W}\}

\displaystyle=-4n^{i}\mathcal{T}-\partial_{A}n^{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\mathcal{T}-n^{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\mathcal{T}+\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}^{i}\,.

(138)

where the nonlinear terms are given by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}}^{i}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}[K_{B}n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}U)]-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}[K^{A}n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)]\,,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}^{i}

\displaystyle=2n^{i}K^{A}K_{A}-n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}K^{C})^{2}+n^{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}K^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}K_{B}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\left(\partial_{A}n^{i}K^{A}K_{A}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}(n^{i}K^{A}K_{A})+4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}K^{C}K^{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}K^{B}K_{B}-\frac{1}{2}K_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}K^{B})]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}n^{i}\Big[5(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)]^{2}-7(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}U)

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}U)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)\Big]+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\Big[\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}U+\delta_{A}^{C}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}U)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\Big[n^{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U+3U)\Big]\,.

(139)

One might wonder whether one can eliminate the remaining non-linear terms
\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}}^{i}
and
\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}^{i}
in the algebra. We show in Appendix
F
that one can eliminate
\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}}^{i}
but that once this is done along the lines we indicate, there is an obstruction to the elimination of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}^{i}
. Furthermore, the elimination of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}}^{i}
itself leads to undesireable features in what concerns the transformation properties of the energy, which would be affected by supertranslation ambiguities (see Section
6
).

5.4
Lorentz transformation of the energy and the linear momentum

It is to be pointed out that when
T
reduces to its zero mode (constant time translations), the non-linear term
\Lambda_{\{b,T\}}
vanishes. The same is true when
W
reduces to its vector harmonic (
k=1
), although it is less immediate to see.

The proof goes as follows. It is convenient to split
\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}
as
follows,

\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}=\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{U}+\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{V}\,,

(140)

where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{U}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\Big[\frac{1}{4}bW\big(5\theta^{2}-7\theta_{AB}\theta^{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta^{BC}\big)

(141)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta-2\theta^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\big)-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\theta_{BC}\theta^{BC}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W\big(2\theta^{2}-3\theta_{BC}\theta^{BC}\big)+\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}W\big(\theta_{A}^{C}\theta_{CB}-\theta_{AB}\theta\big)\Big]\,,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\oint d^{3}x\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Big[bW(2\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\kappa^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa^{rB}+\kappa^{AB}\kappa_{AB})+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}

\displaystyle+4b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W\kappa^{rA}\kappa_{A}^{r}-4b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\kappa_{AB}\kappa^{rB}-\kappa_{B}^{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\kappa^{rB}+\kappa^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa_{A}^{r}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{A}^{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\kappa^{rB}\big)\Big]\,.

First, let us focus on the term
\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{U}
.
We can directly apply the equation for the spatial translations parameter,

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}W=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}W
, on the second order derivative terms
in (
141
). Then, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{U}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[\frac{1}{4}bW\theta^{2}+\frac{1}{4}bW\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{C}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta_{B}^{C}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta-2\theta^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\big)-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\theta_{BC}\theta^{BC}\Big]\,.

(143)

We will make use of the following identity

\displaystyle\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}f\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{C}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta_{B}^{C}\right)

\displaystyle=-\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[\partial_{B}f\left(\theta_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\theta_{C}^{A}-\theta_{C}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\theta_{A}^{B}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+f\left(\theta^{2}-3\theta_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}\right)\Big]\,,

(144)

with
f
an arbitrary function. This can be obtained after integration by parts and taking into account the commutator of two covariant derivatives
[\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}]\theta^{CB}=3\theta^{AB}-\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}
. If we now choose
f=bW/4
, and consider the equations for these parameters
(b,W)
, we have that

\displaystyle\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\frac{bW}{4}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{C}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta_{B}^{C}\right)

\displaystyle=\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[bW\left(\frac{1}{4}\theta^{2}+\frac{5}{4}\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\theta^{2}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}W\left(\theta_{A}^{B}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{C}^{B}\theta_{A}^{C}\right)\Big]\,.

(145)

Replacing this identity in the first line of (
143
), the non-linear term becomes

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{U}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}bW\theta^{2}+\frac{5}{4}bW\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\theta^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}W\theta_{B}^{A}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}W\theta_{C}^{B}\theta_{A}^{C}-\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\theta^{B}_{C}\theta^{C}_{B}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta-2\theta^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\big)\,.

(146)

Integrating by parts the terms proportional to
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}b
in the expression above,
(
146
) reduces to

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{U}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big(b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}W\theta_{C}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta^{BC}-\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W\theta^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\theta\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W\big(\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta-2\theta^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\big)

\displaystyle=0\,,

(147)

which identically vanish after the use of the identity
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\theta_{AC}
.

Let us now proceed to show how the non-linear term
\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{V}
vanishes
for spatial translations. For this, we make use of the definitions of the functions
\kappa^{ij}
in terms of
V
, which are given in (
21
) and (
22
). After some algebra and integration by parts, we get that
\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{V}
becomes

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{V}

\displaystyle=-\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,V\Big[2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\left(W\partial_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V\right)+14\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\left(W\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V\right)

\displaystyle\quad-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\left(bW\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\right)+8\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\left(bW\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\right)-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\left(bW\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V\right)-7\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\left(bW\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V\right)\Big]\,.

(148)

This can again be simplified by using the equation
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}W+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}W=0
. Thus,

\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{V}
reduces to

\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{V}=-\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}V\left[2\left(b\partial_{A}W-\partial_{A}bW\right)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V-8\left(b\partial_{A}W-\partial_{A}bW\right)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\right]\,,

(149)

where we have made use of the identity on the 3-sphere
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V
.

It is straightforward to show that both terms in (
149
) vanish (independently)
after integration by parts and using the equation for
the parameters
b
and
W
. This can be seen for the first term of (
149
), where

\displaystyle 2\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}V\left(b\partial_{A}W-\partial_{A}bW\right)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left(b\partial_{A}W-\partial_{A}bW\right)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(V^{2})

(150)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left(b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}bW\right)V^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

For the second term in (
149
), we proceed in a similar way

\displaystyle\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,V\left(b\partial_{A}W-\partial_{A}bW\right)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\big(b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}bW\big)V\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V

(151)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\left(b\partial_{A}W-\partial_{A}bW\right)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}V\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\left(b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}bW\right)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}V\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

Hence,
\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}^{V}=0
for spatial translations.
Accordingly, we have explicitly shown that the non-linear term
\Lambda_{\{b,W\}}

is also zero for ordinary spatial translations.

It follows from this result that the energy
E
and the linear momentum
P^{i}
transform in the finite-dimensional vector representation of the Lorentz algebra since the non-linear terms appearing in the transformation of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{W}
vanish for them.
E
and
P_{i}
are respectively defined as the values of the generators of constant translations in time (
T=T_{0}
) and space (
W=W_{k=1}
).

6
The importance of the non-linear terms in the energy

The transformation properties of the energy
E
and the linear momentum
P^{i}
under leading and subleading supertranslations can also be directly read off from the algebra, by taking their bracket with the generators of supertranslations. From the fact that the algebra is abelian modulo central terms that vanish for
T_{0}
and
W_{1}
, we simply get that the energy and the linear momentum are invariant,

\delta_{T,W,T^{(1)},I^{(1)}}E=0,\qquad\delta_{T,W,T^{(1)},I^{(1)}}P^{i}=0\,.

(152)

Energy and momentum are thus free from supertranslation ambiguities, as in four dimensions.

This absence of ambiguities seems to be in conflict with the non-invariance of the energy under spatial supertranslations pointed out in
Chrusciel0
. There is in fact no contradiction, because the expression for the energy considered in that reference was the direct generalization to five dimensions of the expression for the energy derived in four dimensions in
Arnowitt:1960zzc
, which is linear. While this expression is correct in four dimensions, it is not so in five dimensions as soon as one takes (as here) boundary conditions compatible with the possibility of performing leading supertranslations. The energy gets additional non-linear contributions,

E=2\oint_{S^{3}_{\infty}}d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left[(1/2)\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}_{A}+D_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+3\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}-(1/8)\theta_{AB}\theta^{AB}+K^{A}K_{A}\right]\,.

(153)

Here the first three linear terms are the direct generalization to five dimensions of the four-dimensional expression of
Arnowitt:1960zzc
(“ADM contribution”), while the other two terms are the non-linear (quadratic) corrections. These non-linear terms acquire non-zero values under supertranslations and are crucial for ensuring the invariance of the energy.

For instance if one performs the coordinate transformation
r=\rho+c
on the flat metric
dr^{2}+r^{2}d\Omega^{2}

where
c
is a constant (see lecture notes
Chrusciel0
, pages 18-19 with
\alpha=1
), which corresponds to a non-trivial leading spatial supertranslation with
W=c
,
one finds after transformation that the fields are given by

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}=c^{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB},\quad\theta_{AB}=2c\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB},\quad\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}=0,\quad\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}=0,\quad K_{A}=0,

(154)

and therefore

E=2\oint_{S^{3}_{\infty}}d^{3}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\gamma$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left(c^{2}-c^{2}\right)=0.

(155)

The first term is the ADM contribution while the compensating second term arises from the non-linear contribution.

The absence of supertranslation ambiguities for the energy and the linear momentum holds only because the supertranslation algebra is abelian modulo central terms that vanish for the energy and the momentum. If the Poisson brackets between the leading supertranslations involved the subleading supertranslations, as did the original choice of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{W}
, then the energy defined as the value of the zero mode
\mathcal{T}_{0}
of the corresponding
\mathcal{T}
would not be invariant under supertranslations. This would mean that the energy could not be identified with the zero mode of that choice of
\mathcal{T}
, but rather of the improved
\mathcal{T}
that fulfills a (centrally extended) abelian algebra. It is for that reason that we redefined
\mathcal{T}
above in order to achieve this property.

7
Matching with null infinity

The Hamiltonian derivation of the ADM-BMS charges given here follows the general approach developed in
Henneaux:2018cst
;
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax
for four dimensions. It is self-contained and makes no reference to null infinity. It is thus independent from the dynamical question of the existence of a smooth null infinity, which is already a non trivial issue in four dimensions
Friedrich2004
;
ValienteKroon:2002fa
. In fact, the parity conditions of
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax
lead to the absence of the leading logarithmic singularities that develop as one goes to null infinity while those of
Henneaux:2018cst
do not eliminate them, but from the point of view of the Hamiltonian formalism and initial data on Cauchy hypersurfaces, this smoothness condition is in fact not a necessary requirement.

The four-dimensional boundary conditions adopted in
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax
leading to the absence of leading logarithmic singularities at null infinity where actually suggested by electromagnetism, where one encounters similar features in a simpler linear context
Henneaux:2018gfi
. There also, appropriate parity conditions on the leading order of the fields at spatial infinity eliminate the leading logarithmic singularities that generically develop at null infinity. In both cases, one can see how logarithmic singularities emerge as one goes to null infinity by (i) integrating the asymptotic equations of motion from spatial infinity to null infinity in hyperbolic coordinates
Ashtekar:1978zz
;
BeigSchmidt
;
Beig:1983sw
; and (ii) following then the methods of Friedrich
Fried1
;
Friedrich:1999wk
;
Friedrich:1999ax
, which are adapted to the “critical sets where the two infinities meet”. The evolution of the leading terms as one goes from spatial infinity to null infinity is governed by the second order Legendre equation
Troessaert:2017jcm
and the parity condition eliminates the term proportional to the Legendre function of the second kind, which becomes singular at the critical sets
Henneaux:2018gfi
;
Henneaux:2018hdj
(see also
Mohamed:2021rfg
).

The approach just outlined has the advantage of matching explicitly the fields at spatial infinity and null infinities (in four dimensions). In particular, one can derive the Strominger’s antipodal matching conditions
Strominger:2013jfa
;
He:2014cra
,
Strominger:2017zoo
from the parity conditions on the initial data of
Henneaux:2018gfi
(for electromagnetism) and
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax
(for gravity). The equality of the past (future) limits of the soft U(1) charges and BMS charges with the corresponding Hamiltonian charges follows from the matching of the fields but is more subtle to establish directly. This is because the natural bases in which the symmetry algebras are given are not the same at null infinity and at spatial infinity, as shown in the earlier analysis of
Troessaert:2017jcm
and further discussed in
Henneaux:2018gfi
;
Henneaux:2018hdj
. [Matching conditions have been derived later from a different perspective in
Prabhu:2019fsp
;
Prabhu:2021cgk
;
Capone:2022gme
, with
Capone:2022gme
emphasizing also the connection between parity conditions and elimination of leading logarithmic singularities.]

Now in five spacetime dimensions, there is no parity condition on the leading orders in the asymptotic expansion of the fields as one goes to spatial infinity. As shown in
Henneaux:2019yqq
for electromagnetism, this leads to more general matching conditions between future and null infinities, involving both odd and even antipodal matchings that turn out to be associated with different inverse powers of
r
as one goes to infinity along null geodesics. The odd matching applies to the leading radiative branch which goes with the fractional power
r^{-\frac{5}{2}}
(in terms of the fields). The even matching – the only one in four dimensions – applies to the Coulomb branch which goes with the power
r^{-3}
. We expect similar features to emerge in gravity. The analysis is harder since spacetime is dynamical and a satisfactory definition of null infinity is more intricate. Work on this question is in progress
prep1
.

8
Conclusions

In this paper, we have given boundary conditions for five-dimensional Einstein gravity at spatial infinity, which define asymptotic flatness. These conditions lead to an infinite-dimensional asymptotic symmetry algebra, which we denoted BMS
5
. The property of this algebra, which turns out to be non-linear in terms of the chosen generators, have been studied.

The boundary conditions are characterized by a relaxation of the decay rate as one goes to spatial infinity with respect to the “Coulomb rate”. The new allowed terms take the form of a
\mathcal{O}(1)
diffeomorphism.
These boundary conditions are rather general, since they contain the Myers-Perry metrics
Myers:1986un
. It is known thanks to the work of
Corvino:2003sp
that under reasonable assumptions, rather general initial data can be put asymptotically to the Myers-Perry form by an appropriate change of coordinates. The coordinate transformation necessary to achieve this task might be improper, just as the boost necessary to bring a black hole at rest would be improper. Our boundary conditions precisely allow for such improper gauge transformations, which it would be incorrect to gauge fix. If we did not include the slowlier decay rate in our boundary conditions, we would see no sign of the supertranslations
Tanabe:2009xb
;
Cameron:2021fhd
.

Our work can be extended in principle to higher dimensions. However, as one increases the dimension, the analysis becomes more and more technically intricate. This is because the gap between the pure (improper) diffeomorphism piece in the expansion of the fields (generated by
\mathcal{O}(1)
vector fields) and the “Coulomb” piece widens by one power of
1/r
as one increases the dimension by one. Non-linearities (of increasing order) then proliferate. Although these non-linearities are not seen in the linear Pauli-Fierz theory, it is nevertherless instructive to carry the analysis in this simplified context. One finds that even though there are potentially more subleading supertranslations associated with the additional intermediate powers of
1/r
in the expansion, preliminary analysis indicates that the size of the BMS group does not increase because the new terms in the diffeomorphism generators define proper gauge transformations. Similar features hold for electromagnetism.

Our analysis raises a number of questions, of which we will only list two.

First, what is the meaning of the enlargement of the supertranslation subalgebra, parametrized now by four independent functions of the angles? What are the corresponding Ward identities and soft theorems? There are indications that a similar enlargement of the supertranslations – and hence the similar problem of understanding the physical implications of these new symmetries – might actually occur already in four dimensions, with the role of the leading supertranslations being played by logarithmic supertranslations, yielding an analogous structure for the supertranslation subalgebra
Chandrasekaran:2021vyu
. More work is required to understand this issue.

Second, while there is an enlargement on the supertranslation side, our analysis does not include the superrotations
Barnich:2010eb
;
Campiglia:2015yka
as asymptotic symmetries. How to include these and their higher dimensional generalization
Capone:2021ouo
in a Hamiltonian treatment at spatial infinity remains an open question.

Appendix A
Action of the diffeomorphisms on the fields (leading orders, unimproved)

We write in this appendix the variations of the leading orders of the fields for a surface deformation (
37
)-(
39
) without the correction terms (i.e., without the ‘more”). We consider successively the space-like diffeomorphisms (
\xi=0
) and the normal ones (
\xi^{i}=0
).

Action on the fields: I. Space-like diffeomorphisms (unimproved)

Space-like diffeomorphisms
(\xi=0)
generate the following transformation laws for the coefficients appearing in the spatial metric fall-off:

•

Leading order (improper gauge part):

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\theta_{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\theta_{AB}+\left(\overline{D}_{A}I_{B}+\overline{D}_{B}I_{A}+2\overline{g}_{AB}W\right)\,,

(156)

where
I_{A}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W
.

•

First subleading order (“core”):

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\overline{\lambda}

\displaystyle=Y^{A}\partial_{A}\overline{\lambda}-W^{(1)}\,,

(157)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\overline{\lambda}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\overline{\lambda}_{A}-\theta_{AB}I^{B}+\overline{D}_{A}W^{(1)}-2I_{A}^{(1)}\,,

(158)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\overline{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\overline{h}_{AB}+\mathcal{L}_{I}\theta_{AB}+W\theta_{AB}+2\left(\overline{D}_{(A}I_{B)}^{(1)}+\overline{g}_{AB}W^{(1)}\right)\,.

(159)

where
\mathcal{L}_{I}
stand for the Lie derivative along the vector

I^{A}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W
.

From
\delta_{\xi}\theta_{AB}
we can read the transformation law for the field
U
, which is

\delta_{\xi^{i}}U=Y^{A}\partial_{A}U+2W\,.

(160)

As mentioned above, an arbitrary term of the form
\Sigma_{\ell,m}\alpha_{\ell,m}Y^{1\ell m}
(spanning the kernel of the operator
\overline{D}_{A}\overline{D}_{B}+\overline{g}_{AB}
) can be added to the transformation law of
U
. This arbitrariness will play no role in the formulas giving the charges, as it should.

The transformation laws of the first terms in the expansion of the momentum are given
by

•

Leading order (improper gauge part)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\kappa^{rr}

\displaystyle=\partial_{A}\left(Y^{A}\kappa^{rr}\right)\,,

(161)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\kappa^{rA}

\displaystyle=\partial_{B}\left(Y^{B}\kappa^{rA}\right)-\partial_{B}Y^{A}\kappa^{rB}\,,

(162)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\kappa^{AB}

\displaystyle=\partial_{C}\left(Y^{C}\kappa^{AB}\right)-\partial_{C}Y^{A}\kappa^{CB}-\partial_{C}Y^{A}\kappa^{CA}\,.

(163)

•

First subleading order (“core”):

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}+\mathcal{L}_{I}\kappa^{rr}-2\kappa^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W+\kappa^{rr}W\,,

(164)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}+\mathcal{L}_{I}\kappa^{rA}+I^{A}\kappa^{rr}-\kappa_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}W\,,

(165)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}+\mathcal{L}_{I}\kappa^{AB}+I^{A}\kappa^{rB}+I^{B}\kappa^{rA}-W\kappa^{AB}\,.

(166)

From these transformation laws, we obtain that the field
V
must transform as

\delta_{\xi^{i}}V=Y^{A}\partial_{A}V\,,

(167)

an expression to which one can add an arbitrary constant, which parametrizes as we pointed out the kernel of the operator relating
\kappa^{ij}
to
V
.

Action on the fields: II. Time-like diffeomorphisms (unimproved)

We will now write down the transformation law of the fields generated by time-like diffeomorphisms
(\xi^{i}=0)
.

The coefficients of the spatial metric fall-off transform as

•

Leading order (improper gauge part)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}\theta_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\overline{g}}}\left(\kappa_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}\overline{g}_{AB}\kappa_{C}^{C}\right)\,.

(168)

but
\delta_{\xi}\theta_{rA}\not=0
(see discussion at the end of this subsection).

•

First subleading order (“core”):

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}\overline{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{b}{3\sqrt{\overline{g}}}\left(2\overline{\pi}^{rr}-\overline{\pi}_{A}^{A}-\theta_{AB}\kappa^{AB}\right)\,,

(169)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}\overline{\lambda}_{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\overline{g}}}\left(\overline{\pi}_{rA}+\theta_{AB}\kappa^{rB}-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{B}^{B}\kappa_{A}^{r}\right)+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{\overline{g}}}\kappa_{A}^{r}\,,

(170)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}\overline{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\overline{g}}}\left[\overline{\pi}_{AB}+2\theta_{C(A}\kappa_{B)}^{C}-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{AB}\kappa_{C}^{C}-\frac{1}{3}\overline{g}_{AB}\left(\overline{\pi}^{rr}+\overline{\pi}_{C}^{C}+\theta_{CD}\kappa^{CD}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\theta_{D}^{D}\left(\kappa_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\kappa_{C}^{C}\right)+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{\overline{g}}}\left(\kappa_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}\overline{g}_{AB}\kappa_{C}^{C}\right)\,.

(171)

The transformation law of the function
U
, which can be consistently extracted from the one of
\theta_{AB}
, is given by

\delta_{\xi}U=2bV

(172)

an expression to which one can again add an arbitrary element of the kernel of the operator that relates
\theta_{AB}
with
U
in (
19
).

Similarly, the transformation laws of the first orders of the momentum in the asymptotic expansion turn out to be given by

•

Leading order (improper gauge part)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}\kappa^{rr}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{\overline{g}}}{2}\left(b\theta_{A}^{A}-\partial_{A}b\overline{D}^{A}\theta_{B}^{B}\right)-\sqrt{\overline{g}}\,\overline{\triangle}T\,,

(173)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}\kappa^{rA}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\overline{g}}}{2}\partial_{B}b\theta^{AB}-\sqrt{\overline{g}}\,\overline{D}^{A}T\,,

(174)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}\kappa^{AB}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\overline{g}}}{2}\left[b\theta^{AB}-\partial_{C}b\overline{D}^{B}\theta^{AC}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\left(b\theta_{C}^{C}-\partial_{C}b\overline{D}^{C}\theta_{D}^{D}\right)\right]-\sqrt{\overline{g}}\left(\overline{g}^{AB}\overline{\triangle}T-\overline{D}^{A}\overline{D}^{B}T\right)\,.

(175)

•

First subleading order (“core”):

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}-3\big)T^{(1)}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}{2}\big(-\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,T+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}T+2\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}T\big)+\frac{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}b\Big(12\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{B}
